role="status"><span><div class="nova-legacy-c-nav__plus-overflow-mount" aria-hidden="true" aria-haspopup="true" aria-expanded="false"></div></span></span></div></div></section></section><div class="nova-legacy-l-flex__item nova-legacy-l-flex nova-legacy-l-flex--gutter-m nova-legacy-l-flex--direction-column@s-up nova-legacy-l-flex--direction-row@xl-only nova-legacy-l-flex--align-items-stretch@s-up nova-legacy-l-flex--justify-content-flex-start@s-up nova-legacy-l-flex--wrap-nowrap@s-up research-detail-middle-section"><div class="nova-legacy-l-flex__item nova-legacy-l-flex__item--grow research-detail-middle-section__item" style="flex-basis:0px;flex-grow:2"><div class="nova-legacy-c-card nova-legacy-c-card--spacing-m nova-legacy-c-card--elevation-1-above"><div class="nova-legacy-c-card__header nova-legacy-c-card__header--show-divider nova-legacy-c-card__header--spacing-inherit"><h2 class="nova-legacy-e-text nova-legacy-e-text--size-l nova-legacy-e-text--family-display nova-legacy-e-text--spacing-none nova-legacy-e-text--color-grey-700">Abstract and Figures</h2></div><div class="nova-legacy-c-card__body nova-legacy-c-card__body--spacing-inherit"><div class="nova-legacy-e-text nova-legacy-e-text--size-m nova-legacy-e-text--family-sans-serif nova-legacy-e-text--spacing-none nova-legacy-e-text--color-grey-800 research-detail-middle-section__abstract" itemProp="description">Though prime numbers cannot be factorized into non-trivial forms, approximate factorizations paves the way to possibly identifying varying respective properties of prime numbers, which could lead to their better understanding and new applications. The present work describes an approach to approximate prime number factorization so as to allow the smallest approximation error. All successive approximate factorizations, starting from two terms up to the smallest possible prime factors (2 and 3), are considered, therefore defining a respective hierarchy, which is represented by a factorization network or binary tree. The length, width, balance, and heterogeneity of the respective binary tree representations are estimated, the latter two measurements being directly related to the complexity of prime numbers. These measurements were used to obtain coincidence similarity networks associated to the approximate factorization. Interestingly, groups of prime numbers are identified in terms of the values of their balance and het-erogeneity indices. In addition, the results obtained indicate that the complexity of prime numbers tends to increase with their magnitude.</div><div class="js-target-figures" id="figures"><div class="nova-legacy-o-grid class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__meta-right"><ul class="nova-legacy-e-list nova-legacy-e-list--size-m nova-legacy-e-list--type-inline nova-legacy-e-list--spacing-none nova-legacy-v-publication-item__meta-data"><li class="nova-legacy-e-list__item nova-legacy-v-publication-item__meta-data-item"><span class="">Mar 2022</span></li></ul></div></div></div><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__stack-item"><ul class="nova-legacy-e-list nova-legacy-e-list--size-m nova-legacy-e-list--type-inline nova-legacy-e-list--spacing-none nova-legacy-v-publication-item__person-list"><li class="nova-legacy-e-list__item"><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-link--theme-bare" href="https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Luciano-Da-F-Costa" type="button"><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item"><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__image-container"><div class="nova-legacy-e-avatar nova-legacy-e-avatar--size-xs nova-legacy-e-avatar--radius-full nova-legacy-e-avatar--framed nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__image"><img src="https://i1.rgstatic.net/ii/profile.image/1084170478596097-1635497793095_Q64/Luciano-Da-F-Costa.jpg" alt="Luciano da F. Costa" class="nova-legacy-e-avatar__img"/></div></span><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__fullname">Luciano da F. Costa</span></span></a></li></ul></div><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__stack-item"><div class="nova-legacy-e-text nova-legacy-e-text--size-m nova-legacy-e-text--family-sans-serif nova-legacy-e-text--spacing-none nova-legacy-e-text--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-text--clamp-3 nova-legacy-v-publication-item__description"><span class="js-target-abstract-6a8e87677a89d lite-page-hidden">Complex networks, which constitute the main subject of network science, have been wide and extensively adopted for representing, characterizing, and modeling an ample range of structures and phenomena from both theoretical and applied perspectives. The present work describes the application of the real-valued Jaccard and real-valued coincidence similarity indices for translating generic datasets into networks. More specifically, two data elements are linked whenever the similarity between their respective features, gauged by some similarity index, is greater than a given threshold. Weighted networks can also be obtained by taking these indices as weights. It is shown that the two proposed real-valued approaches can lead to enhanced performance when compared to cosine and Pearson correlation approaches, yielding a detailed description of the specific patterns of connectivity between the nodes, with enhanced modularity. In addition, a parameter α is introduced that can be used to control the contribution of positive and negative joint variations between the considered features, catering for enhanced flexibility while obtaining networks. The ability of the proposed methodology to capture detailed interconnections and emphasize the modular structure of networks is illustrated and quantified respectively to real-world networks, including handwritten letters and raisin datasets, as well as the Caenorhabditis elegans neuronal network. The reported methodology and results pave the way to a significant number of theoretical and applied developments.</span></div></div></div></div><div Entropy and diversity. Oikos</a></div></div><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__stack-item"><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__meta"><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__meta-left"><span class="nova-legacy-e-badge nova-legacy-e-badge--color-green nova-legacy-e-badge--display-block nova-legacy-e-badge--luminosity-high nova-legacy-e-badge--size-l nova-legacy-e-badge--theme-solid nova-legacy-e-badge--color-green nova-legacy-e-badge--display-block nova-legacy-e-badge--luminosity-medium nova-legacy-e-badge--size-l nova-legacy-e-badge--theme-ghost nova-legacy-e-badge--radius-m nova-legacy-v-publication-item__badge">Full-text available</span></div><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__meta-right"><ul class="nova-legacy-e-list nova-legacy-e-list--size-m nova-legacy-e-list--type-inline nova-legacy-e-list--spacing-none nova-legacy-v-publication-item__meta-data"><li class="nova-legacy-e-list__item nova-legacy-v-publication-item__meta-data-item"><span class="">Feb 2006</span></li><li class="nova-legacy-e-list__item nova-legacy-v-publication-item__meta-data-item"><span class=""><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-link--theme-decorated" href="journal/Oikos-1600-0706">OIKOS</a></span></li></ul></div></div></div><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__stack-item"><ul class="nova-legacy-e-list nova-legacy-e-list--size-m nova-legacy-e-list--type-inline nova-legacy-e-list--spacing-none nova-legacy-v-publication-item__person-list"><li class="nova-legacy-e-list__item"><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-link--theme-bare" href="https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Lou-Jost" type="button"><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item"><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__image-container"><div class="nova-legacy-e-avatar nova-legacy-e-avatar--size-xs nova-legacy-e-avatar--radius-full nova-legacy-e-avatar--framed nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__image"><img src="https://c5.rgstatic.net/m/4671872220764/images/template/default/profile/profile_default_m.jpg" alt="Lou Jost" class="nova-legacy-e-avatar__img"/></div></span><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__fullname">Lou Jost</span></span></a></li></ul></div><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__stack-item"><div class="nova-legacy-e-text nova-legacy-e-text--size-m nova-legacy-e-text--family-sans-serif nova-legacy-e-text--spacing-none nova-legacy-e-text--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-text--clamp-3 nova-legacy-v-publication-item__description"><span class="js-target-abstract-6a8e87677ad62 lite-page-hidden">Entropies such as the Shannon–Wiener and Gini–Simpson indices are not themselves diversities. Conversion of these to effective number of species is the key to a unified and intuitive interpretation of diversity. Effective numbers of species derived from standard diversity indices share a common set of intuitive mathematical properties and behave as one would expect of a diversity, while raw indices do not. Contrary to Keylock, the lack of concavity of effective numbers of species is irrelevant as long as they are used as transformations of concave alpha, beta, and gamma entropies. The practical importance of this transformation is demonstrated by applying it to a popular community similarity measure based on raw diversity indices or entropies. The standard similarity measure based on untransformed indices is shown to give misleading results, but transforming the indices or entropies to effective numbers of species produces a stable, easily interpreted, sensitive general similarity measure. General overlap measures derived from this transformed similarity measure yield the Jaccard index, Sørensen index, Horn index of overlap, and the Morisita–Horn index as special cases.</span></div></div></div></div><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__footer"><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__footer-actions"><div class="nova-legacy-o-pack nova-legacy-o-pack--gutter-m nova-legacy-o-pack--width-auto nova-legacy-o-pack--vertical-align-middle"><div class="nova-legacy-o-pack__item nova-legacy-v-publication-item__footer-button-group"><div class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__fullname">Yan Solihin</span></span></a></li><li class="nova-legacy-e-list__item"><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-link--theme-bare" href="https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Jaejin-Lee-3" type="button"><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item"><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__image-container"><div class="nova-legacy-e-avatar nova-legacy-e-avatar--size-xs nova-legacy-e-avatar--radius-full nova-legacy-e-avatar--framed nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__image"><img src="https://i1.rgstatic.net/ii/profile.image/272455356907520-1441969827024_Q64/Jaejin-Lee-3.jpg" alt="Jaejin Lee" class="nova-legacy-e-avatar__img"/></div></span><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__fullname">Jaejin Lee</span></span></a></li></ul></div><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__stack-item"><div class="nova-legacy-e-text nova-legacy-e-text--size-m nova-legacy-e-text--family-sans-serif nova-legacy-e-text--spacing-none nova-legacy-e-text--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-text--clamp-3 nova-legacy-v-publication-item__description"><span class="js-target-abstract-6a8e87677b228 lite-page-hidden">Using alternative cache indexing/hashing functions is a popular technique to reduce conflict misses by achieving a more uniform cache access distribution across the sets in the cache. Although various alternative hashing functions have been demonstrated to eliminate the worst case conflict behavior, no study has really analyzed the pathological behavior of such hashing functions that often result in performance slowdown. We present an in-depth analysis of the pathological behavior of cache hashing functions. Based on the analysis, we propose two new hashing functions: prime modulo and prime displacement that are resistant to pathological behavior and yet are able to eliminate the worst case conflict behavior in the L2 cache. We show that these two schemes can be implemented in fast hardware using a set of narrow add operations, with negligible fragmentation in the L2 cache. We evaluate the schemes on 23 memory intensive applications. For applications that have nonuniform cache accesses, both prime modulo and prime displacement hashing achieve an average speedup of 1.27 compared to traditional hashing, without slowing down any of the 23 benchmarks. We also evaluate using multiple prime displacement hashing functions in conjunction with a skewed associative L2 cache. The skewed associative cache achieves a better average speedup at the cost of some pathological behavior that slows down four applications by up to 7%.</span></div></div></div></div><div Accordingly, by finding the model which minimizes the description length one obtains estimates of both the integer-valued structure parameters and the real-valued system parameters.</span></div></div></div></div><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__footer"><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__footer-actions"><div class="nova-legacy-o-pack class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-link--theme-bare" href="https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Lucia-Rotger" type="button"><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item"><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__image-container"><div class="nova-legacy-e-avatar nova-legacy-e-avatar--size-xs nova-legacy-e-avatar--radius-full nova-legacy-e-avatar--framed nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__image"><img src="https://i1.rgstatic.net/ii/profile.image/354773569163265-1461596018340_Q64/Lucia-Rotger.jpg" alt="Lucia Rotger" class="nova-legacy-e-avatar__img"/></div></span><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__fullname">Lucia Rotger</span></span></a></li></ul></div><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__stack-item"><div class="nova-legacy-e-text nova-legacy-e-text--size-m nova-legacy-e-text--family-sans-serif nova-legacy-e-text--spacing-none nova-legacy-e-text--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-text--clamp-3 nova-legacy-v-publication-item__description"><span class="js-target-abstract-6a8e87677ad4f lite-page-hidden">Several indices that measure the degree of balance of a rooted phylogenetic tree have been proposed so far in the literature. In this work we define and study a new index of this kind, which we call the total cophenetic index: the sum, over all pairs of different leaves, of the depth of their lowest common ancestor. This index makes sense for arbitrary trees, can be computed in linear time and it has a larger range of values and a greater resolution power than other indices like Colless&#x27; or Sackin&#x27;s. We compute its maximum and minimum values for arbitrary and binary trees, as well as exact formulas for its expected value for binary trees under the Yule and the uniform models of evolution. As a byproduct of this study, we obtain an exact formula for the expected value of the Sackin index under the uniform model, a result that seems to be new in the literature.</span></div></div></div></div><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__footer"><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__footer-actions"><div class="nova-legacy-o-pack class="nova-legacy-v-citation-item__context-source"><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item nova-legacy-v-publication-item--size-m gtm-reference-item"><div><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__stack nova-legacy-v-publication-item__stack--gutter-m"><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__stack-item"><div class="nova-legacy-e-text nova-legacy-e-text--size-l nova-legacy-e-text--family-display nova-legacy-e-text--spacing-none nova-legacy-e-text--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-text--clamp-2 nova-legacy-v-publication-item__title"><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-link--theme-bare" href="publication/221344006_Balanced_binary_trees_for_ID_management_and_load_balance_in_distributed_hash_tables">Balanced binary trees for ID management and load balance in distributed hash tables</a></div></div><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__stack-item"><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__meta"><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__meta-left"><span class="nova-legacy-e-badge nova-legacy-e-badge--color-green nova-legacy-e-badge--display-block nova-legacy-e-badge--luminosity-high nova-legacy-e-badge--size-l nova-legacy-e-badge--theme-solid nova-legacy-e-badge--radius-m nova-legacy-v-publication-item__badge">Conference Paper</span></div><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__meta-right"><ul class="nova-legacy-e-list nova-legacy-e-list--size-m nova-legacy-e-list--type-inline nova-legacy-e-list--spacing-none nova-legacy-v-publication-item__meta-data"><li class="nova-legacy-e-list__item nova-legacy-v-publication-item__meta-data-item"><span class="">Jul 2004</span></li></ul></div></div></div><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__stack-item"><ul class="nova-legacy-e-list nova-legacy-e-list--size-m nova-legacy-e-list--type-inline nova-legacy-e-list--spacing-none nova-legacy-v-publication-item__person-list"><li class="nova-legacy-e-list__item"><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-link--theme-bare" href="https://www.researchgate.net/scientific-contributions/Gurmeet-Singh-Manku-70186099" type="button"><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item"><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__fullname">Gurmeet Singh Manku</span></span></a></li></ul></div><div class="nova-legacy-v-publication-item__stack-item"><div class="nova-legacy-e-text nova-legacy-e-text--size-m nova-legacy-e-text--family-sans-serif nova-legacy-e-text--spacing-none nova-legacy-e-text--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-text--clamp-3 nova-legacy-v-publication-item__description"><span class="js-target-abstract-6a8e87677b0a6 lite-page-hidden">We present a low-cost, decentralized algorithm for ID management in distributed hash tables (DHTs) managed by a dynamic set of hosts. Each host is assigned an ID in the unit interval [0, 1). At any time, the set of IDs splits the interval into disjoint partitions. Hosts do not possess global knowledge of other IDs in the system. The challenge then is to design an efficient decentralized algorithm that maintains roughly equi-sized partitions, in the face of arrivals, departures and changes in the average number of hosts.Our ID management algorithm is the first to enjoy all of the following properties: (a) both arrivals and departures of hosts are handled, (b) departure of a host causes at most one existing host to change its ID, (c) the ratio of the largest to the smallest partition is at most 4, with high probability, and (d) the expected cost per arrival/departure is Θ(R + log n) messages, where n denotes the current number of participants, and R denotes the cost of routing one message in the DHT. In fact, our algorithm is independent of the topology of the overlay network used for routing.Variations of our algorithm diminish the ratio between the largest and the smallest partition to (1 + ε), for any ε &gt; 0, albeit at the cost of re-assigning the IDs of O(1/ε) existing hosts per arrival/departure. Ours is the first algorithm that allows such fine-tuning.Finally, our ID management algorithm enables (a) estimation of the total number of hosts in the system by making only local measurements, and (b) emulation of a variety of deterministic and randomized families of routing topologies, in a straightforward fashion. Among these families are several networks that require O(log n/log k) routing hops in an n-node network with k links per node.</span></div></div></div></div><div role="complementary"><div class="publication-recommendations"><div class="publication-recommendations__outer"><div class="publication-recommendations__inner"><div class="nova-legacy-c-card nova-legacy-e-text--size-l nova-legacy-e-text--family-display nova-legacy-e-text--spacing-xs nova-legacy-e-text--color-inherit publication-recommendation-item__title"><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-link--theme-bare gtm-recommendation-item-link" href="publication/359831098_Retrieving_Hierarchies">Retrieving Hierarchies</a></h3><div class="nova-legacy-e-text nova-legacy-e-text--size-s nova-legacy-e-text--family-sans-serif nova-legacy-e-text--spacing-xs nova-legacy-e-text--color-grey-500"><span>February 2022</span></div><ul class="nova-legacy-e-list nova-legacy-e-list--size-m nova-legacy-e-list--type-inline nova-legacy-e-list--spacing-auto"><li class="nova-legacy-e-list__item"><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item"><span class="lite-person-inline-item__image"><picture class="lite-page-avatar lite-page-avatar--size-xs lite-page-avatar--radius-full"> <img src="data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAAEAAAABCAYAAAAfFcSJAAAADUlEQVR42mN89OjNfwAJDwOxHEacwgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==" data-src="https://i1.rgstatic.net/ii/profile.image/702338843017221-1544462037930_Q64/Alexandre-Benatti.jpg" class="lite-image" alt="Alexandre Benatti"/></picture></span><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__fullname"><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-link--theme-bare" class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item"><span class="lite-person-inline-item__image"><picture class="lite-page-avatar lite-page-avatar--size-xs lite-page-avatar--radius-full"> <img src="data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAAEAAAABCAYAAAAfFcSJAAAADUlEQVR42mN89OjNfwAJDwOxHEacwgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==" data-src="https://i1.rgstatic.net/ii/profile.image/1084170478596097-1635497793095_Q64/Luciano-Da-F-Costa.jpg" class="lite-image" alt="Luciano da F. Costa"/></picture></span><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__fullname"><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-link--theme-bare" href="https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Luciano-Da-F-Costa" type="button">Luciano da F. Costa</a></span></span></li></ul><div class="nova-legacy-e-text nova-legacy-e-text--size-s nova-legacy-e-text--family-sans-serif nova-legacy-e-text--spacing-s nova-legacy-e-text--color-grey-500 publication-recommendation-item__abstract">Several real-world and abstract structures and systems are characterized by marked hierarchy to the point of being represented as trees. Because the study of these entities often involves sampling the tree nodes in a specific order that may not correspond to that originally shaping the tree, reconstruction errors can be obtained. The present work addressed this important problem based on two main <a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-blue nova-legacy-e-link--theme-silent js-lite-click" data-lite="{&quot;click&quot;:[{&quot;t&quot;:&quot;.js-target-6a8e87677b399&quot;,&quot;a&quot;:&quot;tc(lite-page-hidden)&quot;},{&quot;t&quot;:&quot;self&quot;,&quot;a&quot;:&quot;ac(lite-page-hidden)&quot;}]}">... [Show full abstract]</a><span class="lite-page-hidden js-target-6a8e87677b399"> resources: (i) the adoption of a simple model of trees, involving a single parameter; and (ii) the use of the coincidence similarity as the means to quantify the errors by comparing the original and reconstructed structures considering diverse sampling error probability and extent. Several interesting results are obtained and discussed, including the fact that the average reconstruction errors depends weakly on the extent of the errors as well as on the types of trees. At the same time, it is identified that the relative reconstruction accuracy substantially decreases with the error probability.</span></div><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-blue nova-legacy-e-link--theme-silent publication-recommendation-item__more" href="publication/359831098_Retrieving_Hierarchies">View full-text</a></div></div></div></div><div class="nova-legacy-o-grid__column nova-legacy-o-grid__column--width-6/12@m-up publication-recommendation-item__item"><div class="nova-legacy-c-card nova-legacy-c-card--spacing-m nova-legacy-c-card--elevation-none"><div class="nova-legacy-c-card__body nova-legacy-c-card__body--spacing-inherit"><div class="publication-recommendation-item"><div class="nova-legacy-l-flex__item nova-legacy-l-flex nova-legacy-l-flex--gutter-xxs nova-legacy-l-flex--direction-row@s-up nova-legacy-l-flex--align-items-center@s-up nova-legacy-l-flex--justify-content-flex-start@s-up nova-legacy-l-flex--wrap-wrap@s-up publication-recommendation-item__item-badges"><div class="nova-legacy-l-flex__item"><span class="nova-legacy-e-badge nova-legacy-e-badge--color-green nova-legacy-e-badge--size-m nova-legacy-e-badge--theme-ghost nova-legacy-e-badge--radius-m">Full-text available</span></div></div><h3 class="nova-legacy-e-text nova-legacy-e-text--size-l nova-legacy-e-text--family-display nova-legacy-e-text--spacing-xs nova-legacy-e-text--color-inherit publication-recommendation-item__title"><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-link--theme-bare gtm-recommendation-item-link" href="publication/392623253_Expressing_Primes_in_Terms_of_Primes_Are_Prime_Numbers_Modularly_or_Hierarchically_Organized">Expressing Primes in Terms of Primes: Are Prime Numbers Modularly or Hierarchically Organized?</a></h3><div class="nova-legacy-e-text nova-legacy-e-text--size-s nova-legacy-e-text--family-sans-serif nova-legacy-e-text--spacing-xs nova-legacy-e-text--color-grey-500"><span>May 2025</span></div><ul class="nova-legacy-e-list nova-legacy-e-list--size-m nova-legacy-e-list--type-inline nova-legacy-e-list--spacing-auto"><li class="nova-legacy-e-list__item"><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item"><span class="lite-person-inline-item__image"><picture class="lite-page-avatar lite-page-avatar--size-xs lite-page-avatar--radius-full"> <img src="data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAAEAAAABCAYAAAAfFcSJAAAADUlEQVR42mN89OjNfwAJDwOxHEacwgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==" data-src="https://i1.rgstatic.net/ii/profile.image/702338843017221-1544462037930_Q64/Alexandre-Benatti.jpg" class="lite-image" alt="Alexandre Benatti"/></picture></span><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__fullname"><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-link--theme-bare" href="https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Alexandre-Benatti" type="button">Alexandre Benatti</a></span></span></li><li class="nova-legacy-e-list__item"><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item"><span class="lite-person-inline-item__image"><picture class="lite-page-avatar lite-page-avatar--size-xs lite-page-avatar--radius-full"> <img src="data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAAEAAAABCAYAAAAfFcSJAAAADUlEQVR42mN89OjNfwAJDwOxHEacwgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==" data-src="https://i1.rgstatic.net/ii/profile.image/1084170478596097-1635497793095_Q64/Luciano-Da-F-Costa.jpg" class="lite-image" alt="Luciano da F. Costa"/></picture></span><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__fullname"><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-link--theme-bare" href="https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Luciano-Da-F-Costa" type="button">Luciano da F. Costa</a></span></span></li></ul><div class="nova-legacy-e-text nova-legacy-e-text--size-s nova-legacy-e-text--family-sans-serif nova-legacy-e-text--spacing-s nova-legacy-e-text--color-grey-500 publication-recommendation-item__abstract">Prime numbers have motivated interest as a consequence of their irregularity and unpredictability. At the same time, any natural number can be uniquely expressed as a product of the respective prime factors. However, the prime factorization of any prime number yields only itself as a result. In a recent work, an approach (a conjecture) to represent any natural number (including primes) has been <a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-blue nova-legacy-e-link--theme-silent js-lite-click" data-lite="{&quot;click&quot;:[{&quot;t&quot;:&quot;.js-target-6a8e87677b4a9&quot;,&quot;a&quot;:&quot;tc(lite-page-hidden)&quot;},{&quot;t&quot;:&quot;self&quot;,&quot;a&quot;:&quot;ac(lite-page-hidden)&quot;}]}">... [Show full abstract]</a><span class="lite-page-hidden js-target-6a8e87677b4a9"> described, employing the Goldbach to generate even numbers, while an odd number is expressed as a sum of a prime and a double prime (twice the value of a prime number). This representational conjecture paves the way to a study of how primes can be expressed in terms of sums of primes (simple or double), which constitutes the subject of the present work. More specifically, we obtain networks representing the pairwise sum of primes (simple or double), which is found to have a hierarchy of modules (or communities), starting with a division of primes into two main groups which are then further subdivided along successive hierarchies. Other results presented include the number of multiple representations underlying most of the prime numbers.</span></div><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-blue nova-legacy-e-link--theme-silent publication-recommendation-item__more" href="publication/392623253_Expressing_Primes_in_Terms_of_Primes_Are_Prime_Numbers_Modularly_or_Hierarchically_Organized">View full-text</a></div></div></div></div><div class="nova-legacy-o-grid__column nova-legacy-o-grid__column--width-6/12@m-up publication-recommendation-item__item"><div class="nova-legacy-c-card nova-legacy-c-card--spacing-m nova-legacy-c-card--elevation-none"><div class="nova-legacy-c-card__body nova-legacy-c-card__body--spacing-inherit"><div class="publication-recommendation-item"><div class="nova-legacy-l-flex__item nova-legacy-l-flex nova-legacy-l-flex--gutter-xxs nova-legacy-l-flex--direction-row@s-up nova-legacy-l-flex--align-items-center@s-up nova-legacy-l-flex--justify-content-flex-start@s-up nova-legacy-l-flex--wrap-wrap@s-up publication-recommendation-item__item-badges"><div class="nova-legacy-l-flex__item"><span class="nova-legacy-e-badge nova-legacy-e-badge--color-green nova-legacy-e-badge--display-inline nova-legacy-e-badge--luminosity-high nova-legacy-e-badge--size-m nova-legacy-e-badge--theme-solid nova-legacy-e-badge--radius-m">Preprint</span></div><div class="nova-legacy-l-flex__item"><span class="nova-legacy-e-badge nova-legacy-e-badge--color-green nova-legacy-e-badge--display-inline nova-legacy-e-badge--luminosity-medium nova-legacy-e-badge--size-m nova-legacy-e-badge--theme-ghost nova-legacy-e-badge--radius-m">Full-text available</span></div></div><h3 class="nova-legacy-e-text nova-legacy-e-text--size-l nova-legacy-e-text--family-display nova-legacy-e-text--spacing-xs nova-legacy-e-text--color-inherit publication-recommendation-item__title"><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-link--theme-bare gtm-recommendation-item-link" href="publication/373451234_Multilayer_Multiset_Neuronal_Networks_--_MMNNs">Multilayer Multiset Neuronal Networks -- MMNNs</a></h3><div class="nova-legacy-e-text nova-legacy-e-text--size-s nova-legacy-e-text--family-sans-serif nova-legacy-e-text--spacing-xs nova-legacy-e-text--color-grey-500"><span>August 2023</span></div><ul class="nova-legacy-e-list nova-legacy-e-list--size-m nova-legacy-e-list--type-inline nova-legacy-e-list--spacing-auto"><li class="nova-legacy-e-list__item"><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item"><span class="lite-person-inline-item__image"><picture class="lite-page-avatar lite-page-avatar--size-xs lite-page-avatar--radius-full"> <img src="data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAAEAAAABCAYAAAAfFcSJAAAADUlEQVR42mN89OjNfwAJDwOxHEacwgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==" data-src="https://i1.rgstatic.net/ii/profile.image/702338843017221-1544462037930_Q64/Alexandre-Benatti.jpg" class="lite-image" alt="Alexandre Benatti"/></picture></span><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__fullname"><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-link--theme-bare" href="https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Alexandre-Benatti" type="button">Alexandre Benatti</a></span></span></li><li class="nova-legacy-e-list__item"><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item"><span class="lite-person-inline-item__image"><picture class="lite-page-avatar lite-page-avatar--size-xs lite-page-avatar--radius-full"> <img src="data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAAEAAAABCAYAAAAfFcSJAAAADUlEQVR42mN89OjNfwAJDwOxHEacwgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==" data-src="https://i1.rgstatic.net/ii/profile.image/1084170478596097-1635497793095_Q64/Luciano-Da-F-Costa.jpg" class="lite-image" alt="Luciano da F. Costa"/></picture></span><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__fullname"><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-link--theme-bare" href="https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Luciano-Da-F-Costa" type="button">Luciano da F. Costa</a></span></span></li></ul><div class="nova-legacy-e-text nova-legacy-e-text--size-s nova-legacy-e-text--family-sans-serif nova-legacy-e-text--spacing-s nova-legacy-e-text--color-grey-500 publication-recommendation-item__abstract">The coincidence similarity index, based on a combination of the Jaccard and overlap similarity indices, has noticeable properties in comparing and classifying data, including enhanced selectivity and sensitivity, intrinsic normalization, and robustness to data perturbations and outliers. These features allow multiset neurons, which are based on the coincidence similarity operation, to perform <a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-blue nova-legacy-e-link--theme-silent js-lite-click" data-lite="{&quot;click&quot;:[{&quot;t&quot;:&quot;.js-target-6a8e87677b592&quot;,&quot;a&quot;:&quot;tc(lite-page-hidden)&quot;},{&quot;t&quot;:&quot;self&quot;,&quot;a&quot;:&quot;ac(lite-page-hidden)&quot;}]}">... [Show full abstract]</a><span class="lite-page-hidden js-target-6a8e87677b592"> effective pattern recognition applications, including the challenging task of image segmentation. A few prototype points have been used in previous related approaches to represent each pattern to be identified, each of them being associated with respective multiset neurons. The segmentation of the regions can then proceed by taking into account the outputs of these neurons. The present work describes multilayer multiset neuronal networks incorporating two or more layers of coincidence similarity neurons. In addition, as a means to improve performance, this work also explores the utilization of counter-prototype points, which are assigned to the image regions to be avoided. This approach is shown to allow effective segmentation of complex regions despite considering only one prototype and one counter-prototype point. As reported here, the balanced accuracy landscapes to be optimized in order to identify the weight of the neurons in subsequent layers have been found to be relatively smooth, while typically involving more than one attraction basin. The use of a simple gradient-based optimization methodology has been demonstrated to effectively train the considered neural networks with several architectures, at least for the given data type, configuration of parameters, and network architecture.</span></div><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-blue nova-legacy-e-link--theme-silent publication-recommendation-item__more" href="publication/373451234_Multilayer_Multiset_Neuronal_Networks_--_MMNNs">View full-text</a></div></div></div></div><div class="nova-legacy-o-grid__column nova-legacy-o-grid__column--width-6/12@m-up publication-recommendation-item__item"><div class="nova-legacy-c-card nova-legacy-c-card--spacing-m nova-legacy-c-card--elevation-none"><div class="nova-legacy-c-card__body nova-legacy-c-card__body--spacing-inherit"><div class="publication-recommendation-item"><div class="nova-legacy-l-flex__item nova-legacy-l-flex nova-legacy-l-flex--gutter-xxs nova-legacy-l-flex--direction-row@s-up nova-legacy-l-flex--align-items-center@s-up nova-legacy-l-flex--justify-content-flex-start@s-up nova-legacy-l-flex--wrap-wrap@s-up publication-recommendation-item__item-badges"><div class="nova-legacy-l-flex__item"><span class="nova-legacy-e-badge nova-legacy-e-badge--color-green nova-legacy-e-badge--display-inline nova-legacy-e-badge--luminosity-high nova-legacy-e-badge--size-m nova-legacy-e-badge--theme-solid nova-legacy-e-badge--radius-m">Preprint</span></div><div class="nova-legacy-l-flex__item"><span class="nova-legacy-e-badge nova-legacy-e-badge--color-green nova-legacy-e-badge--display-inline nova-legacy-e-badge--luminosity-medium nova-legacy-e-badge--size-m nova-legacy-e-badge--theme-ghost nova-legacy-e-badge--radius-m">Full-text available</span></div></div><h3 class="nova-legacy-e-text nova-legacy-e-text--size-l nova-legacy-e-text--family-display nova-legacy-e-text--spacing-xs nova-legacy-e-text--color-inherit publication-recommendation-item__title"><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-link--theme-bare gtm-recommendation-item-link" href="publication/372986080_Multilayer_Multiset_Neuronal_Networks_-_MMNNs">Multilayer Multiset Neuronal Networks - MMNNs</a></h3><div class="nova-legacy-e-text nova-legacy-e-text--size-s nova-legacy-e-text--family-sans-serif nova-legacy-e-text--spacing-xs nova-legacy-e-text--color-grey-500"><span>July 2023</span></div><ul class="nova-legacy-e-list nova-legacy-e-list--size-m nova-legacy-e-list--type-inline nova-legacy-e-list--spacing-auto"><li class="nova-legacy-e-list__item"><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item"><span class="lite-person-inline-item__image"><picture class="lite-page-avatar lite-page-avatar--size-xs lite-page-avatar--radius-full"> <img src="data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAAEAAAABCAYAAAAfFcSJAAAADUlEQVR42mN89OjNfwAJDwOxHEacwgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==" data-src="https://i1.rgstatic.net/ii/profile.image/702338843017221-1544462037930_Q64/Alexandre-Benatti.jpg" class="lite-image" alt="Alexandre Benatti"/></picture></span><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__fullname"><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-link--theme-bare" href="https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Alexandre-Benatti" type="button">Alexandre Benatti</a></span></span></li><li class="nova-legacy-e-list__item"><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item"><span class="lite-person-inline-item__image"><picture class="lite-page-avatar lite-page-avatar--size-xs lite-page-avatar--radius-full"> <img src="data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAAEAAAABCAYAAAAfFcSJAAAADUlEQVR42mN89OjNfwAJDwOxHEacwgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==" data-src="https://i1.rgstatic.net/ii/profile.image/1084170478596097-1635497793095_Q64/Luciano-Da-F-Costa.jpg" class="lite-image" alt="Luciano da F. Costa"/></picture></span><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__fullname"><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-link--theme-bare" href="https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Luciano-Da-F-Costa" type="button">Luciano da F. Costa</a></span></span></li></ul><div class="nova-legacy-e-text nova-legacy-e-text--size-s nova-legacy-e-text--family-sans-serif nova-legacy-e-text--spacing-s nova-legacy-e-text--color-grey-500 publication-recommendation-item__abstract">The coincidence similarity index, which is based on a multiset extension of the Jaccard and overlap similarity indices, has been found to present especially interesting properties for comparing and classifying data, including enhanced selectivity and sensitivity, intrinsic data normalization, as well as good robustness to data perturbations and outliers. These features allow multiset neurons to <a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-blue nova-legacy-e-link--theme-silent js-lite-click" data-lite="{&quot;click&quot;:[{&quot;t&quot;:&quot;.js-target-6a8e87677b66b&quot;,&quot;a&quot;:&quot;tc(lite-page-hidden)&quot;},{&quot;t&quot;:&quot;self&quot;,&quot;a&quot;:&quot;ac(lite-page-hidden)&quot;}]}">... [Show full abstract]</a><span class="lite-page-hidden js-target-6a8e87677b66b"> be combined in order to perform effective pattern recognition applications, including the challenging task of image segmentation. A few prototype points have been used in previous related approaches to represent each pattern to be identified, each of them being associated with respective multiset neurons. In the present work, multiset neuronal networks are described that can include several layers of coincidence similarity neu-rons. In addition, in order to achieve more effective performance, the possibility to use counter-prototype points is also addressed, which has been found to lead to the impressive segmentation of intricate regions while considering only one prototype and one counter-prototype point. As reported here, the balanced accuracy landscapes to be optimized in order to identify the weight of the neurons in subsequent layers have been found to be relatively smooth, while typically involving more than one attraction basins. A simple gradient-based optimization methodology has been shown to allow, at least for the respective type of data and neuronal network architecture, effective training of the considered networks.</span></div><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-blue nova-legacy-e-link--theme-silent publication-recommendation-item__more" href="publication/372986080_Multilayer_Multiset_Neuronal_Networks_-_MMNNs">View full-text</a></div></div></div></div><div class="nova-legacy-o-grid__column nova-legacy-o-grid__column--width-6/12@m-up publication-recommendation-item__item"><div class="nova-legacy-c-card nova-legacy-c-card--spacing-m nova-legacy-c-card--elevation-none"><div class="nova-legacy-c-card__body nova-legacy-c-card__body--spacing-inherit"><div class="publication-recommendation-item"><div class="nova-legacy-l-flex__item nova-legacy-l-flex nova-legacy-l-flex--gutter-xxs nova-legacy-l-flex--direction-row@s-up nova-legacy-l-flex--align-items-center@s-up nova-legacy-l-flex--justify-content-flex-start@s-up nova-legacy-l-flex--wrap-wrap@s-up publication-recommendation-item__item-badges"><div class="nova-legacy-l-flex__item"><span class="nova-legacy-e-badge nova-legacy-e-badge--color-green nova-legacy-e-badge--display-inline nova-legacy-e-badge--luminosity-high nova-legacy-e-badge--size-m nova-legacy-e-badge--theme-solid nova-legacy-e-badge--radius-m">Preprint</span></div><div class="nova-legacy-l-flex__item"><span class="nova-legacy-e-badge nova-legacy-e-badge--color-green nova-legacy-e-badge--display-inline nova-legacy-e-badge--luminosity-medium nova-legacy-e-badge--size-m nova-legacy-e-badge--theme-ghost nova-legacy-e-badge--radius-m">Full-text available</span></div></div><h3 class="nova-legacy-e-text nova-legacy-e-text--size-l nova-legacy-e-text--family-display nova-legacy-e-text--spacing-xs nova-legacy-e-text--color-inherit publication-recommendation-item__title"><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-link--theme-bare gtm-recommendation-item-link" href="publication/393408989_On_the_Partitioning_Structure_of_Prime_Numbers">On the Partitioning Structure of Prime Numbers</a></h3><div class="nova-legacy-e-text nova-legacy-e-text--size-s nova-legacy-e-text--family-sans-serif nova-legacy-e-text--spacing-xs nova-legacy-e-text--color-grey-500"><span>July 2025</span></div><ul class="nova-legacy-e-list nova-legacy-e-list--size-m nova-legacy-e-list--type-inline nova-legacy-e-list--spacing-auto"><li class="nova-legacy-e-list__item"><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item"><span class="lite-person-inline-item__image"><picture class="lite-page-avatar lite-page-avatar--size-xs lite-page-avatar--radius-full"> <img src="data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAAEAAAABCAYAAAAfFcSJAAAADUlEQVR42mN89OjNfwAJDwOxHEacwgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==" data-src="https://i1.rgstatic.net/ii/profile.image/702338843017221-1544462037930_Q64/Alexandre-Benatti.jpg" class="lite-image" alt="Alexandre Benatti"/></picture></span><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__fullname"><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-link--theme-bare" href="https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Alexandre-Benatti" type="button">Alexandre Benatti</a></span></span></li><li class="nova-legacy-e-list__item"><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item"><span class="lite-person-inline-item__image"><picture class="lite-page-avatar lite-page-avatar--size-xs lite-page-avatar--radius-full"> <img src="data:image/png;base64,iVBORw0KGgoAAAANSUhEUgAAAAEAAAABCAYAAAAfFcSJAAAADUlEQVR42mN89OjNfwAJDwOxHEacwgAAAABJRU5ErkJggg==" data-src="https://i1.rgstatic.net/ii/profile.image/1084170478596097-1635497793095_Q64/Luciano-Da-F-Costa.jpg" class="lite-image" alt="Luciano da F. Costa"/></picture></span><span class="nova-legacy-v-person-inline-item__fullname"><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-inherit nova-legacy-e-link--theme-bare" href="https://www.researchgate.net/profile/Luciano-Da-F-Costa" type="button">Luciano da F. Costa</a></span></span></li></ul><div class="nova-legacy-e-text nova-legacy-e-text--size-s nova-legacy-e-text--family-sans-serif nova-legacy-e-text--spacing-s nova-legacy-e-text--color-grey-500 publication-recommendation-item__abstract">The partition structure of prime numbers is studied by using respective decompositions in terms of a prime and a double prime terms, complementing preliminary results indicating that the prime numbers could be organized into two main groups. Several aspects are described , including the tendency of alternating memberships along the prime sequence, as well as the proposal of a possible algorithm <a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-blue nova-legacy-e-link--theme-silent js-lite-click" data-lite="{&quot;click&quot;:[{&quot;t&quot;:&quot;.js-target-6a8e87677b746&quot;,&quot;a&quot;:&quot;tc(lite-page-hidden)&quot;},{&quot;t&quot;:&quot;self&quot;,&quot;a&quot;:&quot;ac(lite-page-hidden)&quot;}]}">... [Show full abstract]</a><span class="lite-page-hidden js-target-6a8e87677b746"> to identify if a pair of prime numbers belong or not to the same group. This also allowed a procedure to be proposed for identification of the membership of any prime number. These results were then applied to obtain membership signals, which were found to present attenuated lower frequencies, therefore indicating that permanence of the sequence into a same group is less likely than membership alternation. Interestingly, the permanence values were characterized by a well-defined exponential law, supporting the tendency for membership changes along the prime sequence. Further analysis of the membership sequence has been performed in terms of second-order Markov chains, which also indicated preference for alternating sequences. It has also been observed that the proposed membership routine could be applicable to any pair fo prime numbers, and not only successive prime values.</span></div><a class="nova-legacy-e-link nova-legacy-e-link--color-blue nova-legacy-e-link--theme-silent publication-recommendation-item__more" href="publication/393408989_On_the_Partitioning_Structure_of_Prime_Numbers">View full-text</a></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></div></div><div class="nova-legacy-l-flex__item nova-legacy-l-flex nova-legacy-l-flex--gutter-m nova-legacy-l-flex--direction-row@s-up
